Limitless The Way is a limitless vessel; Used by the self, it is not filled by the world; It cannot be cut, knotted, dimmed or stilled; Its depths are hidden, ubiquitous and eternal; I don't know where it comes from; It comes before nature.
Experience Experience is a riverbed, Its source hidden, forever flowing: Its entrance, the root of the world, The Way moves within it: Draw upon it; it will not run dry.
Complete Nature is complete because it does not serve itself. The sage places himself after and finds himself before, Ignores his desire and finds himself content. He is complete because he does not serve himself.
Motion and Use The motion of the Way is to return; The use of the Way is to accept; All things come from the Way, And the Way comes from nothing.
Overcoming Water overcomes the stone; Without substance it requires no opening; This is the benefit of taking no action. Yet benefit without action, And experience without abstraction, Are practiced by very few.
Limitation Who recognizes his limitations is healthy; Who ignores his limitations is sick. The sage recognizes this sickness as a limitation. And so becomes immune.

Truly, only acting without thought of one's life is superior to valuing one's life. [Tao Te Ching chapter 75]
Truly, only those who see illness as illness can avoid illness. [Tao Te Ching chapter 71]
Use justice to rule a country. Use surprise to wage war. Use non-action to govern the world. [Tao Te Ching chapter 57]
Use the light to return to clarity. [Tao Te Ching chapter 52]
Warriors say: I dare not be like the host, but would rather be like the guest. I dare not advance an inch, but would rather retreat a foot. [Tao Te Ching chapter 69]
Wary, as if surrounded by strangers. [Tao Te Ching chapter 15]
We go from birth to death. Three out of ten follow life. Three out of ten follow death. People who rush from birth to death are also three out of ten. Why is that so? Because they want to make too much of life. [Tao Te Ching chapter 50]
Weapons are ominous tools. They are abhorred by all creatures. Anyone who follows the Way shuns them. [Tao Te Ching chapter 31]
Weapons are ominous tools. They are not the noble ruler's tools. He only uses them when he can't avoid it. [Tao Te Ching chapter 31]
Wear down to be renewed. [Tao Te Ching chapter 22]
What Heaven detests, who knows why? Even the sage considers it difficult. [Tao Te Ching chapter 73]
What has no substance can penetrate what has no opening. [Tao Te Ching chapter 43]
What has not yet emerged is easy to prevent. [Tao Te Ching chapter 64]
What is and what is not create each other. [Tao Te Ching chapter 2]
What is well held will not escape. [Tao Te Ching chapter 54]
What is well planted will not be uprooted. [Tao Te Ching chapter 54]
What people loathe the most is to be orphaned, desolate, unworthy. But this is what princes and kings call themselves. [Tao Te Ching chapter 42]
What should be abolished must first be cherished. [Tao Te Ching chapter 36]
What should be deprived must first be enriched. [Tao Te Ching chapter 36]
What should be shrunken must first be stretched. [Tao Te Ching chapter 36]
What should be weakened must first be strengthened. [Tao Te Ching chapter 36]
What the Way is to the world, the stream is to the river and the sea. [Tao Te Ching chapter 32]
What's the difference between beautiful and ugly? [Tao Te Ching chapter 20]
What's the difference between yes and no? [Tao Te Ching chapter 20]
When bitter enemies make peace, surely some bitterness remains. [Tao Te Ching chapter 79]
When equal armies battle, the grieving one will be victorious. [Tao Te Ching chapter 69]
When everyone sees good, then bad exists. [Tao Te Ching chapter 2]
When family ties are disturbed, devoted children arise. [Tao Te Ching chapter 18]
When leading people and serving Heaven, nothing exceeds moderation. [Tao Te Ching chapter 59]
When many people are killed, they should be mourned and lamented. Those who are victorious in war should follow the rites of funerals. [Tao Te Ching chapter 31]
When much virtue is achieved, nothing is not overcome. [Tao Te Ching chapter 59]
When nothing is done, nothing is left undone. [Tao Te Ching chapter 48]
When people are unsettled, loyal ministers arise. [Tao Te Ching chapter 18]
When people do not dread authorities, then a greater dread descends. [Tao Te Ching chapter 72]
When the Way governs the world, the proud stallions drag dung carriages. When the Way is lost to the world, war horses are bred outside the city. [Tao Te Ching chapter 46]
When the Way is lost there is virtue. When virtue is lost there is benevolence. When benevolence is lost there is righteousness. When righteousness is lost there are rituals. [Tao Te Ching chapter 38]
When the emperor is crowned or the three dukes are appointed, rather than sending a gift of jade carried by four horses, remain still and offer the Way. [Tao Te Ching chapter 62]
When the government is quite unobtrusive, people are indeed pure. When the government is quite prying, people are indeed conniving. [Tao Te Ching chapter 58]
When the great Way is abandoned, benevolence and righteousness arise. [Tao Te Ching chapter 18]
When the palace is magnificent, the fields are filled with weeds, and the granaries are empty. [Tao Te Ching chapter 53]
When the sage stands above people, they are not oppressed. When he leads people, they are not obstructed. The world will exalt him and not grow tired of him. [Tao Te Ching chapter 66]
When the uncarved wood is split, its parts are put to use. When the sage is put to use, he becomes the head. [Tao Te Ching chapter 28]
When the world is ruled according to the Way, the ghosts lose their power. The ghosts do not really lose their power, but it is not used to harm people. [Tao Te Ching chapter 60]
When wisdom and knowledge appear, great pretense arises. [Tao Te Ching chapter 18]
Where there is no conflict, there is no fault. [Tao Te Ching chapter 8]
Who can rest until the moment of action? [Tao Te Ching chapter 15]
Who can wait in stillness while the mud settles? [Tao Te Ching chapter 15]
Who has excess and supplies the world? Only the one who follows the Way. [Tao Te Ching chapter 77]
Why did the ancients praise the Way? Did they not say it was because you find what you seek and are saved from your wrongdoings? [Tao Te Ching chapter 62]
Without desire there is stillness, and the world settles by itself. [Tao Te Ching chapter 37]
Without looking through the window, you can see Heaven's Way. [Tao Te Ching chapter 47]
Without stepping out the door, you can know the world. [Tao Te Ching chapter 47]
Words spoken about the Way have no taste. When looked at, there's not enough to see. When listened to, there's not enough to hear. When used, it is never exhausted. [Tao Te Ching chapter 35]
Yielding is the manner of the Way. [Tao Te Ching chapter 40]
Yielding, like ice about to melt. [Tao Te Ching chapter 15]
